\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)=\frac{1}{\omega}\cdot(\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)).

Since the morphism
\tau_{1}^{L}E\to\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
is a monomorphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
by Lemma
3.9
,
the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
implies

(4.3)

\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\leq\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}\left(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\right)=\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

Moreover
there exists a subobject
\tilde{F}
of
E
in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
such that

\tau_{1}^{L}\tilde{F}\cong\tau_{1}^{L}E
and
\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F})\cong\tau_{1}^{L}E
by Lemma
2.25
and Remark
2.26
.
The
\mathit{M}_{\beta,\omega}^{Z_{1},Z_{2}}
-semistability of
E
implies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(\tilde{F})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)\leq 0
.

Now the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
implies

\displaystyle\mu_{\beta,\omega}(\tilde{F})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}\tilde{F})-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F})+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F})}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}\tilde{F})+\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}\tilde{F})-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F}))+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F}))}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}\tilde{F})+\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}\tilde{F}))}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)}{2\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)}\leq 0.

Since
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)
is positive, we see

(4.4)

\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\right)\leq\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E).

Thus (
4.2
), (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) imply

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(E)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta}{\omega}(\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E))+\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta^{2}+\omega^{2}}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

\displaystyle\geq\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta^{2}+\omega^{2}}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\omega}\left(\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\right)+\frac{\beta}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\frac{\beta^{2}+\omega^{2}}{\omega}\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+\beta)^{2}+\omega^{2}}{\omega}\cdot\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)>0.

∎

Remark 4.3
.

Suppose that a semiorthogonal decomposition
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
satisfies
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
If stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
satisfy
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
,
then
\sigma_{2}
is
\sigma_{1}[-1]
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Suppose stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
are rational and satisfy
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
If
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
are rational and both
\omega
and
\beta
are rational then
the pair
{{\Sigma}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}=({{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}},{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}})
is a locally finite stability condition.

Proof.

Since
\sigma_{i}
is rational,
\sigma_{i}
is discrete.
In particular the heart
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
of
\sigma_{i}
is Noetherian and so is

{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
.

Suppose
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}
satisfies
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(E)=0
.
Due to
[
CP10
, Lemma 3.4]
, it is enough to show that any increasing filtration
E^{1}\subset E^{2}\subset\cdots\subset E^{n}\subset\cdots
of subobject of
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}
terminates.
Note that
E^{n}
also satisfies
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(E^{n})=0
.

Taking the cohomology with respect to
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
, we have a sequence of monomorphisms

H^{-1}(E^{n})\subset H^{-1}(E^{n+1})\subset H^{-1}(E).

We can assume
H^{-1}(E^{n})
does not depend on
n
since
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
is Noetherian.
Now we claim
H^{0}(E^{n})
satisfies

(4.5)

\displaystyle\tau_{2}^{R}H^{0}(E^{n})

\displaystyle=0,\text{ and}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\tau_{1}^{L}H^{0}(E^{n})

\displaystyle\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\sigma_{1}}.

Indeed, by the vanishing
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(E^{n})=0
,
we have
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(H^{0}(E^{n}))=0
which implies
that
H^{0}(E^{n})
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-torsion.
The vanishing
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(H^{0}(E^{n}))=0
and the definition of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}

imply
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}H^{0}(E^{n}))=0
and
\tau_{2}^{R}H^{0}(E^{n})=0
.
This gives the proof of the claim.

Let
F^{n}
be the quotient
E^{n}/E^{n-1}
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}
.
Since
H^{-1}(E^{n})
is constant for
n
, we have a monomorphism in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

\textstyle{H^{-1}(F^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(E^{n-1}).}

Thus
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{\mathsf{gl}\left({Z_{1}},{Z_{2}}\right)}\left(H^{-1}(F^{n})\right)=0
holds by (
4.5
) and (
4.6
).
On the other hand, if
H^{-1}(F^{n})
is non-zero, then
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits{\mathsf{gl}\left({Z_{1}},{Z_{2}}\right)}\left(H^{-1}(F^{n})\right)
is positive by the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-freeness of
H^{-1}(F)
.
So
H^{-1}(F^{n})
has to be
0
and the canonical morphism

H^{0}(E^{n-1})\to H^{0}(E^{n})
is a monomorphism in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
.

To complete the proof, it is enough to show that
H^{0}(E^{n})
is independent of
n
.
By (
4.5
) and (
4.6
), we have
\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(H^{0}(E^{n}))=\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{R}H^{0}(E^{n}))<0
,
it is enough to show that
\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(H^{0}(E^{n}))
is bounded below.

Let
G^{n}
be the quotient
E/E^{n}
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}
.
Then we have the following exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{-1}(E^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{-1}(E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{n}}
\textstyle{H^{-1}(G^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{n}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(E^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{n}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{n}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(G^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Since
H^{-1}(E^{n})=\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\varphi_{n}
is independent of
n
, so is
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi_{n}=\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n}
.
Moreover we may assume that
\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\pi_{n}=\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\psi_{n}
is independent of
n
since
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
is Noetherian.
Then it is enough to show that
\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})
is bounded below since
H^{0}(E^{n})
is an extension by
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\psi_{n}
and
\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(E^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\psi_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Note that
\tau_{2}^{R}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})=0
since the functor
\tau_{2}^{R}
is
t
-exact with respect to
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
.
Consider the exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{-1}(G^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Then Lemma
2.20
implies the following diagram of distinguished triangle in
{\mathbf{D}}_{1}
:

(4.7)

\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{R}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{R}(H^{-1}(G^{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{R}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{L}(H^{-1}(G^{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Phi(H^{-1}(G^{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

The second and third row give short exact sequences in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.
Since
\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n}
and
H^{-1}(G^{n})
are in
{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}
,
the morphism
\nu
and
\epsilon
are mono morphisms in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
by Lemma
3.9
.
Thus we see the following isomorphism

\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})\cong\frac{\tau_{1}^{L}(H^{-1}(G^{n}))}{\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})}\cong\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\left(\frac{\Phi(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})}{\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})}\to\frac{\Phi(H^{-1}(G^{n}))}{\tau_{1}^{L}(H^{-1}(G^{n}))}\right).

The
t
-exactness of
\tau_{1}^{L}
with respect to
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
implies

\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})\subset\tau_{1}^{L}(H^{0}(E^{n}))
.
Since
H^{0}(E^{n})
satisfies (
4.5
) and (
4.6
),
\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})
is in
{\mathcal{T}}^{\sigma_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}^{R}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})=0
.
Moreover
\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})
is a subobject of the torsion part
T
of
{\Phi(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})}/{\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})}
with respect to the torsion pair
({\mathcal{T}}^{\sigma_{1}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\sigma_{1}})
.

Thus we see
{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})=Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n}))
and

(4.8)

\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(T)\leq\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})=\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n}).

Since
\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n}
is independent of
n
, so is
\Phi(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})/\tau_{1}^{L}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\delta_{n})
.
Hence
\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}(\mathop{\mathsf{ker}}\nolimits\psi_{n})
is bonded below
and the pair
{{\Sigma}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}=({{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}},{{Z}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}})
is a stability condition on
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Finally by the rationality of
\sigma_{i}
and
(\beta,\omega)
, the stability condition
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
is rational.
Since
K_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
is finitely generated, a rational stability condition is reasonable and in particular is locally finite.
∎

5.
Deformation property

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Fixing
\sigma_{i}\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
,
we have constructed a family
\{{{\Sigma}_{\beta,\omega}^{{\sigma_{1}},{\sigma_{2}}}}\mid\beta\in{\mathbb{Q}},\omega\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{>0}\}
of locally finite stability conditions on
{\mathbf{D}}
.
Now we wish to extend the family for real numbers and to show that the family is continuous for
\beta
and
\omega
.

Although one of standard solutions is the support property of
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
we show that
the central charge
{{Z}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
for
(\beta^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
satisfies a finiteness condition

(5.1)

\|{{Z}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\|_{{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}}<\infty

which has been established in Bridgeland’s deformation of stability conditions (see also Theorem
2.10
).
To show this, by the definition of
{{Z}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, it is enough to show that
the supremum

(5.2)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|E\text{ is ${{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}$-semistable}\right\}

is finite.
This will be proven in Proposition
5.15
.
In the proposition, we only prove (
5.2
) for points
(\beta,\omega)
in a contractible open set
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
of the upper half-plane
{\mathcal{H}}
.
The restriction to
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
is necessary for Proposition
5.15
by a technical reason.

A key ingredient of Proposition
5.15
is Corollary
2.2
.
If the supremum of
|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|
for any semistable object
E
was smaller than
\pi
,
then the desired assertion followed from Corollary
2.2
.
However, we do not see whether the canonical decomposition

\textstyle{i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{1}\tau_{1}^{L}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E[1]}

satisfies the desired property or not since the decomposition is too “rough”.

Thus a finer decomposition of the semistable object
E
is necessary.
Roughly “finer decomposition” reflect the following principle:

•

For a
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free object
F\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
, if the difference

\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F)-\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F)
is sufficiently small, then
the difference

|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)|

satisfies the assumption in Corollary
2.2
.

To complete Proposition
5.15
,
it is necessary to observe properties of
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistable object.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Set a full subcategory of
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
by

(5.3)

{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{>1}=\{E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}\mid\text{the $(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})$-free part $F$ of $E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}$ satisfies $\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F)>1$}\}.

(1)

For any
E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{>1}
, the inequality
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)\geq\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
holds.

(2)

The supremum

(5.4)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{>1}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

Let
T
be the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-torsion part of
E
and
F
the quotient
E/T
in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
.
The definition of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
implies

(5.5)

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)\leq\pi.

The assumption
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F)>1
implies
\tau_{1}^{L}F=0
by the assertion (3) in Proposition
3.6
.
Hence we have

\mu_{\beta,\omega}(F)=\frac{-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)+\beta Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)}>1

which implies
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\left((\beta-1-\sqrt{-1}\omega)Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)\right)>0
.
Thus we have

(5.6)

\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)=\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F).

Since
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)
is nothing but

{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)+{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)
,
the inequalities (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) give the proof of the assertion (1).

Since
F
satisfies
\tau_{1}^{L}F=0
, we have
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)=\pi
unless
\tau_{1}^{L}E
is zero.
Hence there exists a
\theta\in(0,\pi)
such that
the inequality

(5.7)

0\leq|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|\leq\theta

for any
E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{>1}
.
Then Corollary
2.2
gives the proof of (2).
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
so that
\epsilon_{2}\leq\epsilon_{1}<1
.
Suppose a
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free object
E
satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)<1
.

(1)

Suppose
\tau_{1}^{L}E\neq 0
.
If
E
satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)\leq\epsilon_{1}
, then

\arg Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\leq\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)

for any
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.

(2)

If
E
satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)\leq\epsilon_{1}
, then

\arg Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\leq\arg(\beta-\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)

for any
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.

(3)

If
E
satisfies
\epsilon_{2}\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)
then

\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
.

Proof.

We first note that
\tau_{2}^{R}E
is non-zero and in particular
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\neq 0
.
Otherwise,
E
is in the essential image of
i_{1}\colon{\mathbf{D}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{D}}
and hence
E
is
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistable with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)=1
by Proposition
3.6
.
This gives a contradiction.

Since
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)<1
, the morphism
\tau_{1}^{L}E\to\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
is a monomorphism in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
by Lemma
3.9
.
Moreover Lemma
2.25
(and the proof) implies that there exists a subobject
F\subset E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
such that
\tau_{1}^{L}F\cong\tau_{1}^{L}E
and
\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}F)\cong\tau_{1}^{L}E
.
Since the subobject
F
also satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F)\leq\epsilon_{1}
by the assumption for
E
, we see:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)+\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}F))+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}F))}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)+\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}F))}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+(\beta+1)\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)}{2\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)}.

The last inequality implies

(5.8)

-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1})\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\leq 0.

Since the left hand side of (
5.8
) is just the imaginary part of
(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1}-\sqrt{-1}\omega)Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)
,
this gives the proof of the assertion (1).

Note that
i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E
is a subobject of
E
in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
.
Hence
i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E
also satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E)\leq\epsilon_{1}
.
So we see

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(i_{2}\tau_{2}^{R}E)=\frac{-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)}.

Thus we have

(5.9)

-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+(\beta-\epsilon_{1})\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\leq 0

which gives the proof of the assertion (2).

We show the assertion (3).
By the assumption
\epsilon_{2}\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)
, we see

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}(\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)+\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E))

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

\displaystyle\iff(\epsilon_{2}-1)\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)

\displaystyle\leq-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+(\beta-\epsilon_{2})\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E).

Lemma
3.9
and the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
implies
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\leq\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{1}(\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E))=\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
.
Hence we obtain

(5.10)

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{2}-1)\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

\displaystyle\leq-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+(\beta-\epsilon_{2})\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\iff 0

\displaystyle\leq-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)+(\beta-2\epsilon_{2}+1)\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E).

By the same argument for (1), we see

\arg(\beta-2\epsilon_{2}+1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
which satisfy

0\leq\epsilon_{1},0<1-2\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}\leq\epsilon_{1}<1,\text{ and }

(5.12)

0<\omega^{2}+(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2})^{2}+2(1-2\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1}).

Suppose that a
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free object
E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
satisfies

(5.13)

\epsilon_{2}\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)\leq\epsilon_{1}.

(1)

There exists a
\theta\in(0,\pi)
such that
the argument of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)
satisfies

0<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)-\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\theta_{0}

for any
E
with the condition (
5.13
).

(2)

The supremum

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|E\text{ is $(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})$-free with }\epsilon_{2}\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E)\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)\leq\epsilon_{1}\right\}.

is finite.

Proof.

Set
\theta_{1}=\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
,

\theta_{2}=\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
,
and

\theta_{3}=\arg(\beta-\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.
Then the conditions
\epsilon_{1}>0
and
1-2\epsilon_{2}>0
imply that

\theta_{1}
is in the open interval
(0,\pi)
,
\theta_{2}
is non-positive, and
\theta_{3}
is in
[0,\pi)
.

Lemma
5.2
implies the following:

(5.14)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)\leq\theta_{1},\text{ and }

(5.15)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}

\displaystyle<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\leq\theta_{3}.

We claim that the assumption for
(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
implies

(5.16)

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}<\pi.

Note that the inequalities (
5.16
) is equivalent to

(5.17)

\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\frac{\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega}{(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)}>0.

Since the left hand side of (
5.17
) is

\omega\cdot(\omega^{2}+(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2})^{2}+2(1-2\epsilon_{2})(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1}))
up to the positive constant

|(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)|^{2}
,
the inequalities (
5.16
) hold.

Now the inequalities

(5.18)

0<\theta_{3}-\theta_{2}<\pi

also hold as follows.
Since
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits(\beta-\epsilon_{1}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)/(\beta+1+2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
is
\omega(1+\epsilon_{1}-2\epsilon_{2})
up to positive constant, the assumption for
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
imply

\displaystyle 1+\epsilon_{1}-2\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1+\epsilon_{1}-2\epsilon_{1}=1-\epsilon_{1}>0.

The inequalities above imply (
5.18
).

Set
\theta_{0}
by
\max\{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2},\theta_{3}-\theta_{2}\}
.
Since
\theta_{2}
is negative,
the arguments
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E)
and
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
are in the interval

(\theta_{2},\theta_{0}+\theta_{2}]
.
By inequalities (
5.16
) and (
5.18
),
\theta_{0}
is in
(0,\pi)
. Moreover (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) implies

\theta_{2}<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)\leq\theta_{0}+\theta_{2}.

Then Corollary
2.2
implies the assertion (2).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

If
\epsilon_{1}=0
, then the inequality (
5.12
) is nothing but

(5.19)

\omega^{2}+\beta^{2}+(2\beta+1)(2\epsilon_{2}+1)\geq 0.

Thus
(\beta,\omega)
satisfies the (
5.19
) if
2\beta+1\geq 0
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.

For an
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
, set an open subset
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})
by

(5.20)

{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})=\left\{(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\middle|\begin{split}0&<\omega^{2}+(\beta+1)^{2}-2\epsilon_{1},\text{ and }\\
0&<\omega^{2}+(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{1})^{2}+2(1-2\epsilon_{1})(\epsilon_{1}-1)\end{split}\right\}.

Set a full subcategory
{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{<1}
of
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
by

(5.21)

{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{<1}=\{E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}\mid E\text{ is $(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})$-free with }0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(E)\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)<1\}.

If
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})
,

(1)

then there exists a
\theta\in(0,\pi)
such that the following holds:

\sup\{\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)\mid E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{<1}\}\leq\theta.

(2)

The supremum

(5.22)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{<1}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

Taking the Harder-Narasimhan filtration with respect to
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability, we obtain a short exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

(5.23)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
E^{+}
and
E^{-}
satisfy the following:

•

E^{+}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\epsilon_{1}<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(E^{+})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E^{+})<1
and

•

E^{-}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(E^{-})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E^{-})\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

Now we note that two inequalities in (
5.20
) come from the inequality (
5.12
) in Lemma
5.3
.
Applying Lemma
5.3
, there exist
\theta_{+}
and
\theta_{-}
in
(0,\pi)
such that
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{i})\leq\theta_{i}
where
i\in\{+,-\}
.
If set
\max\{\theta_{+},\theta_{-}\}
by
\theta
,
then the argument of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)={{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})+{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})
is in the interval
(0,\theta]
and this gives the proof of the assertion (1) since
\theta
is independent of the choice of
E\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{<1}
.

Both
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})
and
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})
are positive
since
\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E^{+})
and
\mu_{\beta,\omega}(E^{-})
are positive.
Moreover the inequality
|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})-{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|\leq\theta
holds.
Then Proposition
2.1
implies that there exists a positive constant
C>0
such that the following holds:

(5.24)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|\geq C|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|+C|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|.

By Lemma
5.2
, there exist
\theta_{0}
and
\theta_{0}^{\prime}
in the interval
(0,\pi)
such that
the following hold:

(5.25)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E^{+})-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{+})|\leq\theta_{0},\text{ and}

(5.26)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E^{-})-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{-})|\leq\theta_{0}^{\prime}.

Hence we see

\displaystyle\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C}\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{+})+{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|}\right)

Corollary
2.2
implies that
both
{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{+})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{+})|}
and

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{-})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-})|}
are bounded above by (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).
Hence the supremum is bounded above.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

In the final section, we wish to deform the stability condition
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
along a path from
(1,0)\in\overline{{\mathcal{H}}}
to
(\cos 2\pi/3,\sin 2\pi/3)\in{\mathcal{H}}
.
If set
\epsilon_{1}
by
1/3
, then the set
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(1/3)
includes the desired path.

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
\epsilon_{2}\leq 0
and
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathbb{Q}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}_{>0}
so that

(5.27)

2\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}>0.

Set
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\leq\epsilon_{2}}
by

{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\leq\epsilon_{2}}:=\{F\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}\mid F\text{ is $(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})$-free with }\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F)\leq\epsilon_{2}\}

and take
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\leq\epsilon_{2}}
.

(1)

The argument of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)
is greater than
\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.
In particular the following holds for any
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\leq\epsilon_{2}}
:

(5.28)

\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)\leq 0

(2)

The supremum

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)|}\middle|F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\leq\epsilon_{2}}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

The imaginary part
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\frac{\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega}{\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega}

is
\omega(2\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2})
up to positive constant.
Then
The assumption
2\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}>0
directly implies

(5.29)

0<\arg\frac{\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega}{\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega}<\pi.

By the definition of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, we have
\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)
.
Lemma
5.2
implies

(5.30)

0<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}F)\leq\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega),\text{and}

(5.31)

\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)\leq\arg(\beta-\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Since
\arg(\beta-\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
is negative,
the inequalities (
5.29
), (
5.30
), and (
5.31
) imply

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)<{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)
. In addition the inequalities
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F)\leq\epsilon_{2}<0
imply
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)\leq 0
, and thus we have

(5.32)

\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)\leq 0.

Finally, by (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), Corollary
2.2
implies the assertion (2).
∎

The following lemma might be technical for readers, but is necessary for the proof of Proposition
5.9

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathbb{Q}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}_{>0}
.

Suppose that
E\in{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
satisfies the following:

•

\tau_{1}^{L}E
is
\sigma_{1}
-free, and

•

the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free part
E_{{\mathrm{fr}}}
of
E
has
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(E_{{\mathrm{fr}}})\geq 1
.

The the following holds:

(1)

The
\sigma_{2}
-free part
F
of
\tau_{2}^{R}E
has the property
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(i_{2}F)\geq 1
.

(2)

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\geq\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.

Proof.

Let
E_{{\mathrm{tor}}}
be the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-torsion part of
E
.
By the assumption we see
\tau_{1}^{L}E_{{\mathrm{tor}}}=0
and

(5.33)

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E_{{\mathrm{tor}}})=\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E_{{\mathrm{tor}}})=\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Let
A
be an arbitrary subobject of
\tau_{2}^{R}E
such that the quotient
\tau_{2}^{R}E/A
is
\sigma_{2}
-free.
Then
i_{2}A
gives a subobject of
E
.
Hence the quotient
Q=E/i_{2}A
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(Q)\geq 1
.
Then the inequalities
1\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(Q)\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}(Q)
imply

(5.34)

-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)+(\beta-1)\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)\geq 0,

which means
\arg Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)\geq\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.
The inequality (
5.34
) also implies

(5.35)

\frac{-\omega\mathop{\mathfrak{Re}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)+\beta\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)}{\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}Q)}\geq 1.

Since
A
is arbitrary, the inequality (
5.35
) implies the assertion (1).

Recall
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)={{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E_{{\mathrm{tor}}})+{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(i_{2}F)

and
Q=E/i_{2}
.
Applying the inequality (
5.34
)
A
as
\sigma_{2}
-torsion part of
\tau_{2}^{R}E
,
we have

(5.36)

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(i_{2}F)=\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)Z_{2}(F)\geq\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Then (
5.33
) and (
5.36
) imply the assertion (2).
∎

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
for an
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
(cf. (
5.20
)).

Then the supremum

(5.37)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|}\middle|T\in{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\cap{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\phi)\text{ where
}\pi\phi\in(0,\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)]\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

We first note that the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-torsion part
E_{{\mathrm{tor}}}
of any
E\in{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
satisfies

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E_{{\mathrm{tor}}})\leq\pi

by the definition of
{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Moreover the equality
\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)={{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E_{{\mathrm{tor}}})
holds if and only if

\tau_{1}^{L}E_{{\mathrm{tor}}}=0
.
Thus, for an object
T\in{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\cap{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\phi)
with
\pi\phi\in(0,\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)]
, we may assume that
\tau_{1}^{L}T
is
\sigma_{1}
-free
since
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)\leq\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.

Taking the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
T
with respect to
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability,
we have the following short exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

(5.38)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
T^{+}
and
T^{-}
satisfy

•

the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free part
T^{+}_{{\mathrm{fr}}}
of
T^{+}
has
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(T^{+}_{{\mathrm{fr}}})\geq 1
, and

•

T^{-}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(T^{-})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(T^{-})<1
.

Note that
T^{-}
also satisfies
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})>0
. By Lemma
5.5
, there exists a
\theta
in
(0,\pi)
such that the inequalities

(5.39)

0<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})\leq\theta

holds for any
T
.

Now the sequence (
5.38
) gives an exact sequence not only in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
but also in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Thus
T^{+}
is a subobject of
T
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
and
T^{+}
satisfies

(5.40)

0<\phi^{-}(T^{+})\leq\phi^{+}(T^{+})\leq\phi^{+}(T)\leq\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)

with respect to the stability condition
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

By Proposition
2.1
,
there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
which is independent of
T
such that the inequality below holds:

(5.41)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|\geq C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|+C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|.

Then, the inequality (
5.41
) implies the following:

(5.42)

\displaystyle\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|}\right)

(5.43)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|}\right)

(5.44)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|}\right).

By Proposition
5.5
, there exists a positive constant
M_{1}>0
such that
the following holds:

(5.45)

\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{-})|}\leq M_{1}

Taking semiorthogonal decomposition of
T^{+}
, there exists a distinguished triangle below:

(5.46)

\textstyle{\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{1}^{L}T^{+}.}

Since
\tau_{1}^{L}T_{+}
is a subobject of
\tau_{1}^{L}T
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
,

\tau_{1}^{L}T_{+}
is
\sigma_{1}
-free.
Then, by Lemma
5.8
,
\tau_{2}^{R}T
is in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
and the inequality

(5.47)

\theta_{1}\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})

holds where
\theta_{1}=\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.
In parituclar the distinguished triangle (
5.46
)
gives a short exact sequence not only in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
but also in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Hence
\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+}
is a subobject of
T
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, and the inequalities

(5.48)

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})\leq\pi\phi\leq\theta_{2}:=\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)

holds.

Now the closed interval
[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]
is contained in
(0,\pi]
.
Since
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}T^{+})
is in the interval
(0,\pi]
,
there exists a
\theta\in[0,\pi)
such that the inequalities

0\leq|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}T^{+})-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|\leq\theta

hold for any
T
.
By Corollary
2.2
,
there exists a positive constant
M_{2}
which is independent of the choice of
T
and satisfies the following:

(5.49)

\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{+})|}=\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}T^{+})+{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{+})|}\leq M_{2}.

Thus we have finished the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
For an
\epsilon_{2}<0
and set a subset

{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
of
{\mathcal{H}}
by

(5.50)

{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2}):=\left\{(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathcal{H}}\middle|\begin{split}0&<2\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2},\text{ and}\\
0&<\omega^{2}+(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2})^{2}+2\epsilon_{2}(1-2\epsilon_{2})\end{split}\right\}.

Suppose that
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
.
Then the supremum

(5.51)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)|}\middle|F\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

Choose
F\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Taking the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
F
with respect to
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability,
we have the following short exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
F^{+}
and
F^{-}
satisfy

•

F^{+}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\epsilon_{2}<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F^{+})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{+})\leq 0
, and

•

F^{-}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{-})\leq\epsilon_{2}
.

Since
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{+})\leq 0
, applying Lemma
5.3
to
F^{+}
,
there exists a
\theta_{+}\in(-\pi,0)
such that the inequalities hold:

(5.52)

\theta_{+}<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})\leq 0.

Moreover, applying Corollary
5.7
to
F^{-}
, there exists a
\theta_{-}
in the open interval
(-\pi,0)
such that the following holds:

(5.53)

\theta_{-}\leq{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})\leq 0.

Thus one can use Proposition
2.1
by (
5.52
) and (
5.53
) and there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that
the following holds:

(5.54)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)|\geq C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|+C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|.

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
5.9
, we see

\displaystyle\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{+})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{-})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|}\right)

Now, by Lemma
5.3
and Corollary
5.7
, there exist
upper bounds for
{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{+})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})|}
and

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}F^{-})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})|}
.
This gives the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
(\beta,\omega)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})
for an
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
.

Set
{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\prime}
by

(5.55)

{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\prime}={{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\cap{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}\left[\frac{1}{\pi}\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega),1\right]

Then the supremum

(5.56)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|}\middle|T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\prime}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

Taking the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\beta,\omega}^{\prime}
with respect to
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability,
there exists a short exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{>1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\leq 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

such that

•

the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free part
T^{>1}_{{\mathrm{fr}}}
of
T^{>1}
satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(T^{>1}_{{\mathrm{fr}}})>1
, and

•

T^{\leq 1}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(T^{\leq 1})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(T^{\leq 1})\leq 1
.

Then Lemma
5.1
implies

(5.57)

\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1}).

Moreover the canonical morphism
T\to T^{\leq 1}
is an epimorphism not only in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
but also in

{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Hence the quotient
T^{\leq 1}
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
also satisfies

(5.58)

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1}).

Thus, by Proposition
2.1
,
there exists a constant
C_{1}
such that
the following holds:

(5.59)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|\geq C_{1}\left(|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1})|\right)

Taking the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
T^{\leq 1}
, we have the short exact sequence in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{=1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{\leq 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{<1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
T^{=1}
and
T^{<1}
are

•

T^{=1}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free and is
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistable with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}(T^{=1})=1
, and

•

T^{<1}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(T^{<1})<1
.

Note that the inequality
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(T^{<1})>0
also holds.
Then the canonical morphism
T\to T^{<1}
gives an epimorphism in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Thus
T^{<1}
satisfies

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})
.
By Proposition
5.5
, there exists a
\theta_{1}\in(0,\pi)
such that the inequality

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})\leq\theta_{1}
holds.
Thus we obtain

(5.60)

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})\leq\theta_{1}

and we see that there exists a
\theta\in[0,\pi)
such that
|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|<\theta
.
By Proposition
2.1
,
there exists a positive constant
C_{2}
such that the following holds:

(5.61)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1})|\geq C_{2}\left(|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|\right).

Thus we see

\displaystyle\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{\leq 1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{\leq 1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{\leq 1})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{\leq 1})|}{C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})|+C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{=1})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{<1})|}{C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})|+C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|}\right)

(5.62)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{=1})|}{C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{<1})|}{C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|}\right)

By Lemma
5.1
, there exists an upper bound for

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{>1})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{>1})|}
.
The assumption
\mu_{\beta,\omega}(T^{=1})=1
implies
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{=1})=\arg(\beta-1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
and hence
the difference

|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{1}^{L}T^{=1})-\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{=1})|

is strictly smaller than
\pi
.
Hence there exists an upper bound for
{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{=1})|}/{C_{2}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{=1})|}
by Corollary
2.2
.
Finally, by Lemma
5.3
, there exists an upper bound for

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}T^{<1})|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(T^{<1})|}
.
Hence (
5.62
) is bounded above.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
If
(\beta,\omega)
is in
{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
then the inequality

\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)<{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F[1])

holds for any
F\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

Proof.

Taking the Harder-Narashimhan filtration of
F\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
we obtain the short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
F^{+}
and
F^{-}
satisfy

•

F^{+}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\epsilon_{2}<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F^{+})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{+})\leq 0
, and

•

F^{-}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{-})\leq\epsilon_{2}
.

In particular we have

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{\pm})\leq 0
.

Since
\epsilon_{2}
is negative, Lemma
5.3
implies

(5.63)

\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{+})\leq 0.

In addition Corollary
5.7
implies

(5.64)

\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F^{-})\leq 0.

Since the inequality
\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<\arg(\beta+1-2\epsilon_{2}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)
clearly holds,
we obtain

\arg(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)<{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F)\leq 0

which gives the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Suppose that
(\beta,\omega)
is in
{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
,

Let
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
be
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
-semistable with the phase
\phi
.
If
\pi\phi\leq\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
, then
E
is in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
In particular,

{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(0,\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)/\pi]
is the full subcategory of
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

Proof.

Since the pair
({{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}},{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}[1])
is a torsion pair on

{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, there is an short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
T\in{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
and
F\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
By the
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
-semistability of
E
, we have

(5.65)

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(F[1])\leq\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)\leq\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Then the inequalities (
5.65
) contradict Lemma
5.12
.
Hence
F[1]
has to be zero.
The last assertion follows from the definition of
{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(0,\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)/\pi]
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.

Then the supremum

(5.66)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|E\in{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\phi)\text{ where }\pi\phi\in\left(\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega),\pi\right]\right\}

is finite if
(\beta,\omega)
is in
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
.

Proof.

Let
E
be in the slicing
{{{\mathcal{P}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\phi)
where
\pi\phi
is in
\left(\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega),\pi\right]
.
Since
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, there exists a canonical distinguished triangle

\textstyle{E^{-1}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{0}}

where
E^{0}\in{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
and
E^{-1}\in{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Then
E^{-1}
satisfies
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])>\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
by Lemma
5.12
.
Since the morphism
E\to E^{0}
is an epimorphism in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, we see

(5.67)

\phi(E^{0})\geq\phi^{-}(E^{0})\geq\phi^{-}(E)=\phi(E)\geq\frac{1}{\pi}\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Hence inequalities
0\leq|\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})-{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|<\pi
hold and there exists a positive constant
C_{1}
such that the inequality

(5.68)

|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|\geq C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|+C_{1}|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|

holds for any
E
.
Then we see

\displaystyle\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{0})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}(E^{-1}[1]))|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{0})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}(E^{-1}[1]))|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|+|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1}}\left(\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{0})|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|}+\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}(E^{-1}[1]))|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}\right).

Since
E^{-1}
is in
{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
, Proposition
5.10
implies that

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}(E^{-1}[1]))|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{-1}[1])|}
is bounded above.
Moreover, by Proposition
5.11
,

{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}(E^{0}))|}/{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E^{0})|}
is bounded above.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.

If
(\beta,\omega)
be in
\left({\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})\right)_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
then the stability condition
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
satisfies
that the supremum

(5.69)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|\text{$E$ is ${{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}$-semistable}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

Let
E
be a
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
-semistable object with the phase
\phi
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\phi\in(0,1]
.

If
\pi\phi\leq\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
, then
E
is in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
by Proposition
5.13
.
Then, by Proposition
5.9
,
there exists an
M_{1}>0
such that

(5.70)

\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}<M_{1}.

Suppose
\pi\phi>\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)
.
By Proposition
5.14
, we see the supremum

(5.71)

\sup\left\{\frac{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|\text{$E$ is ${{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}$-semistable with }\pi\phi>\arg(-\beta+\sqrt{-1}\omega)\right\}

is bounded.
Hence we have finished the desired assertion.
∎

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.

If a rational points
(\beta,\omega)
is in
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})
, then the supremum

(5.72)

\sup\left\{\frac{|Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|}{|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)|}\middle|\text{$E$ is ${{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}$-semistable}\right\}

is finite.

Proof.

By the definition, we see
|{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|=|\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega|\cdot|Z_{2}(\tau_{2}^{R}E)|
for any
E\in{\mathbf{D}}
.
Thus Proposition
5.15
implies the desired assertion.
∎

Now we will show that the following map

(5.73)

s_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\colon{\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2})_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}}},s_{{\mathbb{Q}}}(\beta,\omega)={{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}

is continuous.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Suppose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
satisfies
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
and
E^{\prime}\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
where
(\beta,\omega)
and
(\beta^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.
Then the vanishing
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(E[p],E^{\prime})=0
holds for any
p\geq 2
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}^{\sigma_{i}}
be the slicing of
\sigma_{i}
.
By the construction of the heart
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
if
E
is in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
then

\tau_{1}^{L}E\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\sigma_{1}}(0,2]
and
\tau_{2}^{R}E\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\sigma_{2}}(0,2]
.
Since
\sigma_{i}
satisfies
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{3}})}
, we see
\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E)\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\sigma_{1}}(0,2]
.
Thus the vanishings

(5.74)

\begin{cases}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E[p],\tau_{1}^{L}E^{\prime})=0&\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}(\tau_{2}^{R}E[p],\tau_{2}^{R}E^{\prime})=0&\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}}(\tau_{1}^{L}E[p],\Phi(\tau_{2}^{R}E^{\prime})[-1])=0\end{cases}

hold.
Then Lemma
2.21
implies the desired assertion.
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}={\left\langle{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}},{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}\right\rangle}
be a semiorthogonal decomposition with
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{2}})}
.
Choose rational stability conditions
\sigma_{i}=({\mathcal{A}}_{i},Z_{i})\in{\mathsf{Stab}\,{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}
such that
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
satisfies the condition
\mathop{(\mathrm{Mor{5}})}
.
Take a
(\beta,\omega)\in({\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2}))_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
for an
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
and an
\epsilon_{2}<0
.

For an arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
choose
(\beta^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})\in({\mathcal{H}}^{+}(\epsilon_{1})\cap{\mathcal{H}}^{-}(\epsilon_{2}))_{{\mathbb{Q}}}
so that the following holds:

(5.75)

\sup\{|\mu_{\beta^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}^{+}(E)-\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(E)|,|\mu_{\beta^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}^{-}(E)-\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(E)|\mid E\text{ is }(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\text{-free}\}<\varepsilon.

(1)

Let
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
be
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
-semistable with

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E)>\arg(\beta+1+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega)+\pi

Then the vanishing
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(E[1],E^{\prime})=0
holds for any
E^{\prime}\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

(2)

Let
E\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
be
{{\Sigma}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
-semistable with

\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(E[-1])\leq\arg(\beta+1+2\epsilon_{0}+\sqrt{-1}\omega)(\beta-\sqrt{-1}\omega).

Then the vanishing
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(E^{\prime},E[-1])=0
holds for any
E^{\prime}\in{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof,
let
T
(resp.
T^{\prime}
) be the free part of
E
(resp.
E^{\prime}
) and let
F[1]
(resp.
F^{\prime}[1]
) be the torsion part of
E
(resp.
E^{\prime}
) with respect to
the torsion pair
({{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}[1],{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}})
on the heart

{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
(resp. on
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
).
Then one easily see

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(E[1],E^{\prime})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(T,F^{\prime})
.

Basically the proof of the assertions are similar.
Since
F^{\prime}
is in
{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{{\beta^{\prime}},{\omega^{\prime}}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
we have
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{\prime})\leq\epsilon
by (
5.75
).
By truncating the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
F^{\prime}
with respect to the
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability,
there exists a subobject
F^{\prime}_{+}
of
F^{\prime}
in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
such that

•

F^{\prime}_{+}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with
0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(F^{\prime}_{+})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{\prime}_{+})\leq\epsilon
,

•

the quotient
F^{\prime}_{-}:=F^{\prime}/F^{\prime}_{+}
in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with

\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(F^{\prime}_{-})\leq 0
.

Then any morphism
\varphi\colon T\to F^{\prime}
factors through
F^{\prime}_{+}
, that is,
we have the following diagram:

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}.}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Note that
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
is not only the free part of
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
but also the torsion part of
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
.
Since the morphism
\varphi^{\prime}\colon T\to F^{\prime}_{+}
above lives in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
which is closed under subobjects in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
the image
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}
of
\varphi^{\prime}
in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
belongs to
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.

Again by truncating the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}
with respect to the
\mu_{\beta,\omega}
-semistability,
we obtain a subobject
G
of
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}
in the abelian category
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
such that

•

the
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free part
G_{{\mathrm{fr}}}
of
G_{+}
satisfies
\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(G_{{\mathrm{fr}}})>\epsilon
, and

•

the quotient
G_{-}:=\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}/G_{+}
is
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
-free with

0<\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{-}(G_{-})\leq\mu_{\beta,\omega}^{+}(G_{-})\leq\epsilon
.

Thus we have the diagram:

\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{-}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}_{+}}

The vanishing
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{D}}}(G_{+},F^{\prime}_{+})=0
gives the lift of
\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}\to F^{\prime}_{+}
to
G^{-}
.
The horizontal line gives a short exact sequence not only in
{\mathsf{gl}\left({{\mathcal{A}}_{1}},{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}\right)}
but also in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}

since all objects are in
{{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
.
Hence the composite
E\to T\to\mathop{\mathsf{im}}\nolimits\varphi^{\prime}\to G_{-}
gives an epi morphism in
{{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}
,
in particular
G_{-}
is a quotient of
E
.
So the argument
\arg{{Z}_{{\beta},{\omega}}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}}(G^{-})
should satisfy

(5.76)
